##### **DetourBench: Trajectory-Level Evaluation of Interactive Agents with Exact Minimum-Action Bounds**
2611.20844v1 by Hanlin Tian, Sihan Zhu, Zhao Yang, Yuxiang Wang, Zhiquan Hu, Yu Mi, Hongquan Zhu, Qiufei Hu

Benchmarks judge language-model agents mainly by whether they reach the correct final state, not by how they got there. Endpoint scoring cannot separate an agent that retrieved exactly the evidence a decision required from one that read most of the environment, nor an agent that never located the evidence from one that stopped a single action short. Settling this needs a reference cost, and authored solutions establish only that a task is solvable, not that no shorter route exists.
  We present DetourBench, which constrains the environment until that minimum is computable. Each task is a finite, partially observable transition system with unit-cost accesses whose resources become globally addressable after discovery. Exhaustive search yields the full-information minimum action count $L_0$ with a replayable witness; from graph-recoverable terminal states it also yields exact residual work $R_T$, which separates an episode that stopped one access short from one that wandered, while an irreversible wrong commitment is recorded as having no finite residual distance rather than assigned one. Success, attainment, residual progress, and normalized detour are reported separately. Across 375 scored episodes --- five model endpoints, 25 integrated tasks, three repeats --- success ranges from 58.7% to 96.0% while conditional attainment ranges from 0.803 to 0.890 and orders the models differently. Among recoverable failures, residual work separates near-complete trajectories from costly incompletion and from irreversible commitment. We open-source the instances, oracle witnesses, and evaluation harness at https://github.com/DatagridsAI/DetourBench.

##### **Instant Weight-Space Consolidation for Long-Term Memory in LLMs**
2611.20759v1 by Manan Suri, Dinesh Manocha

Agentic memory systems give LLMs persistent memory by extracting experience into text and retrieving it at query time, but the model itself does not evolve: however many interactions it has had, it starts each query the same. Extraction errors compound, implicit cross-session patterns that no single fragment states cannot be surfaced, and the construction cost grows with every conversation. Fine-tuning on past interactions does change the weights, but requires backpropagation at serving time and degrades on long histories. We introduce CLAM (Carried Latent Adapter Memory), which combines the strengths of both: a recurrent hypernetwork performs parametric consolidation, compiling a stream of session activations into a low-rank adapter in a single forward pass with no gradient steps and no language-model generation calls, while a complementary episodic buffer supplies verbatim detail at inference. A gated latent state carries knowledge across sessions in fixed size, so the parametric memory accumulates without growing. On LoCoMo and LongMemEval-S across three open models (2-7B parameters), CLAM leads all agentic baselines by 1-45 points and all gradient-based parametric methods by 10-45 points, while requiring zero generative LLM calls, zero gradient steps, and 3-151x less wall-clock time than extraction systems to build its memory. The two channels are complementary: the consolidated adapter adds 12-15 accuracy points when the episodic buffer misses the gold evidence, and still adds 4-5 points even with an oracle buffer.

##### **LEAF-OPD: Learning to Forget from Privileged Contexts via On-Policy Distillation**
2611.20674v1 by Zirui Pang, Zhijie Deng, Yuancheng Wang, Qihan Yu, Xin Li, Jiaheng Wei, Zixin Zhong

On forget data, the teacher uses privileged contexts (instructions not to reveal knowledge about forget targets) to guide forgetting. On retain data, the teacher provides supervision without privileged context to preserve model utility. The student operates without privileged context in both cases, learning how to respond when encountering knowledge that should be forgotten. A key challenge is that the teacher often provides effective supervision only at the first few tokens of student trajectories. We introduce Supervision Signal (SS) to measure corrective supervision over a specified token interval, evaluating the entire trajectory (SS1) and its second half (SS0.5) to assess overall strength and late-stage persistence. On RWKU, it outperforms the second-best method by 34% in Forget Quality while maintaining competitive Retain Quality. ##### **Abstention Direction in Large Language Models: Mechanistic Interpretability of Abstention Across Query Ambiguity, Knowledge Limits, and Safety Alignment**
2611.20441v1 by Koki Itai, Jun Hirako, Hiroaki Yamagiwa

Abstention, the withholding of a response by a large language model (LLM) under uncertainty or safety risks, is essential for trustworthy models. The causes of abstention fall into three perspectives: ambiguity in the Query (Q), limits of Model Knowledge (MK), and misalignment with Human Values (HV). However, prior analyses of internal representations have focused on HV, using a refusal direction computed from harmful and harmless queries. No study has analyzed the three perspectives jointly. We therefore extend the refusal direction framework to all three perspectives. Specifically, for each perspective, we construct counterfactual pairs that share a template and differ only in the abstention-inducing factor, and compute an abstention direction by normalizing the difference in mean activations between the two polarities. Activation steering experiments show that intervening in middle layers increases or decreases abstention across all three perspectives. Moreover, steering yields cross-perspective effects, indicating that the abstention directions share a common directional component.

##### **A Structural Framework for Succession: Continuation, Recognition, and Realization**
2611.20422v1 by Ilya A. Shkrob

Succession asks whether an entity encountered later is the same entity that entered an arrangement earlier. For an artificial system that is fine-tuned, distilled, or copied, it has to be answered. We look for a mathematical structure weak enough to cover any such case and strong enough to support general theorems, so that results follow from the structure's conditions rather than from a particular pipeline or criterion. Succession is modeled on state/modification graphs with relational lifts that compose along paths. Within this framework we separate four questions that are often conflated: which continuations a representation admits, which descendants are compatible with a modification, which are recognized as continuing the identity, and which are realized. The framework allows one to address systematically questions of symmetry, computational complexity, recoverability, and robustness to partial realization, and we establish what the structure settles in each case before any particular criterion of identity is chosen.

Gemma achieved higher response-quality, context-retention, and safety scores; Qwen offered faster generation and lower memory use. Clinician assessment of 150 Gemma responses across nine clinical domains yielded a mean quality score of 3.80/4, with 147 responses meeting the acceptance criterion. A paired RAG-on/off experiment evaluated 74 questions: retrieval improved 11 responses, left 56 neutral, produced four adverse response changes, and left three pairs unassessable. RAG-on had descriptively higher mean usefulness, safety, correctness, and actionability in the conditional 70-pair subset excluding the four flagged pairs from both conditions. Physical-device testing demonstrated offline responses on a 6 GB Android handset, with a 14.3 s median latency, alongside successful reconnection synchronization in the integrated application. Together, the findings document clinical response quality and practical performance of the integrated offline maternal-health assistant.

##### **Interactive Structured Environment Scaling for Large Language Model Agentic Reasoning**

Reinforcement learning via environmental interaction is a cornerstone post-training paradigm for evolving large language models into autonomous agents capable of multi-turn tool use and exploration. Because the environment defines what the policy observes and how actions are verified, its structure directly bounds the capabilities an agent can acquire. Yet existing structured and retrieval environments rely on upstream pre-filtering---extracting a compact subgraph around the gold answer or retrieving fixed passages before the policy acts. We show that such pre-filtering introduces a fundamental measurement confound: because per-question subgraphs enclose ground-truth paths, they compress the candidate space by orders of magnitude and leak answer locations via local topology, allowing a question-blind degree heuristic to surface gold answers far above chance. To restore path selection, candidate pruning, and optimal stopping as genuine policy decisions, we propose RISE (Interactive Structured Environment), which transitions structured reinforcement learning from static subgraphs to full-scale interactive databases along two coupled axes. First, Decision-Space Scaling exposes the unpruned knowledge graph via a five-stage graph-agnostic typed intent adapter under a bounded call budget and a multiplicative grounding reward gate. Second, Learning-Frontier Scaling overcomes within-group variance collapse under group-normalized objectives by uniting model-independent structural tier progression with a self-evolving competence-frontier bandit. Across nineteen benchmarks and three model backbones, RISE substantially advances in-domain and unseen-schema knowledge-graph reasoning, transfers zero-shot to open-domain passage retrieval, and significantly enhances out-of-domain mathematical and logical deduction.

##### **BadGCL: Towards Persistent Backdoor Attack on Graph Continual Learning Systems**
2611.19656v1 by Dong Li, Yufei Han, Xintao Wu, Xujiang Zhao, Wei Cheng, Chen Zhao

This work reveals a contrasting vulnerability: knowledge-preservation mechanisms in GCL can also preserve early injected backdoors when the poisoned update is compatible with the clean-task signal being retained. For graph data, this risk is amplified by structural triggers, which are embedded into the message-passing paths of graph neural networks (GNNs) and can induce stable representation shifts across tasks. To expose this risk, we propose BadGCL, a model-agnostic framework for persistent backdoor attacks under a strict early-stage intervention setting. ##### **GPBam: A Retrieval-Aware German Public Law Exam Benchmark**
2611.18981v1 by Abdullah Al Zubaer, Lorenz Wendlinger, Simon Alexander Nonn, Michael Granitzer, Jelena Mitrović

Legal reasoning in civil-law systems is not rule-lookup: the German state examination asks for a structured opinion (Gutachten) that names the governing norms, applies them to the facts, and argues to a conclusion. We introduce GPBam (German Public Law Bar-style Exam), 81 public-law examination cases with reference solutions, paired with a recitation task over 200 statutory provisions. Drawn from state examination preparation, the cases are broad and rarely repeat, and are not publicly available - we find no evidence of exact-solution memorisation in the models we evaluate, and keeping them private limits contamination. Because each reference solution's cited norms form a retrieval gold set over the very instances the essays are graded on, GPBam scores retrieval and long-form generation jointly, which makes it a benchmark for legal retrieval as well as for legal writing and lets us ask how much retrieval quality a legal opinion actually needs. Across 32 open-weight and proprietary models evaluated without retrieval, scores span 4.81-76.42 out of 100. Supplying the law helps modestly: across seven models and eight retrieval configurations, retrieval performance varies nearly threefold and buys at most 4.1 essay points, and nothing significant for three of the seven, while an oracle with 91% gold-norm recall improves scores by 2-6 points. Essay quality strongly tracks prior knowledge: verbatim statute recall, measured in a separate task, correlates at $ρ= 0.92$ over 32 models.

##### **Context Matters: Improving LLM-Based Code Summarization with Hierarchical Information**
2611.18964v1 by Alexandre Petrachini, Breno Bernard Nicolau de França, Guido Araujo

Source code documentation plays an important role in software quality activities, such as code review, program comprehension, maintenance, and bug fixing. However, it is often fragmented across individual code elements and focuses on local implementation details, providing little information about their surrounding context. This limits both developers' understanding of software systems and the effectiveness of Large Language Models (LLMs), whose reasoning is constrained by finite context windows. Enriching documentation with hierarchical context has the potential to improve software comprehension and support downstream, quality-related tasks for both developers and LLM-based tools. In this paper, we present an in silico study that showcases the impact of augmenting context on the generation of code-level documentation, using a variety of LLMs. By varying the function, class, and module hierarchy visible to the LLMs, we assess whether this variation produces a significant difference in the resulting code documentation compared with the existing documentation, which serves as a proxy for developer intent. Our results show that Documentation-Aware prompts with class or module-level context consistently yield summaries that are closest to the original documentation. Adding surrounding documentation yields statistically significant improvements over the baseline across models, with effect sizes ranging from negligible (DeepSeek) through weak (GPT-4o-mini) to medium (Gemini). We restrict the study to this immediate hierarchical context; call-graph traversal and cross-module dependency context fall outside our scope and are left to future work. These findings provide a clear path to improve automated documentation tools and enhance LLMs' ability to reason about codebases for downstream tasks, such as code retrieval.

##### **From Language-Specific to Cross-DSL: Semantic-Role-Guided Model Completion with LLMs**
2611.18952v1 by Meriem Ben Chaaben, Théo Giraudet, Benoit Combemale, Houari Sahraoui

The rapid emergence of large language models (LLMs), trained on vast and diverse corpora, has positioned them as a promising source of domain knowledge to assist modelers. However, leveraging LLMs for model completion remains challenging: suggestions must respect both the syntax of a domain-specific language (DSL) and the intended domain semantics, while remaining consistent with an evolving model. This paper presents RAFiC, an LLM-based approach for iterative domain model completion that generalizes across DSLs. It drives completion using metamodel information to ensure syntactic validity and enriches prompts with semantic-role annotations to better capture the intended modeling role of DSL elements. The approach provides context-aware, incremental guidance during model construction. We evaluate RAFiC through an ablation-based comparative study over three DSLs and two scenarios per DSL to isolate the contribution of syntactic and semantic guidance. Across these six scenarios, semantic role guidance improves contribution rate in every case, and RAFiC improves completion effectiveness over weaker prompting configurations in most of them. The largest gains occur for DSLs that are less represented in LLM training data. In these cases, iterative guidance leads to earlier and more stable accepted suggestions, while semantic role guidance improves their relevance.
